Xiong C., Melenbrink N.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Xiong, Chenjie (60623275300); Melenbrink, Nathan (56712952800)
60623275300; 56712952800
Environmentally Responsive Shading Structures with Sensor-Based Prefabricated Mechanical Systems
(2026) ASHRAE Transactions, 132 (1), pp. 1444 - 1451, Cited 0 times.
DOI: 10.63044/w26xio184
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105038361506?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: Harvard University, Graduate School of Design, Cambridge, MA, United States; Harvard University, Faculty of Arts and Science, Cambridge, MA, United States
ABSTRACT: Climate change has intensified the global demand for cooling solutions, particularly in regions experiencing extreme heatwaves. Kinetic building façades act as a shading structure, dynamically adjusting their shape according to environmental conditions to improve energy efficiency and occupant comfort. However, traditional application has largely been limited to commercial and office buildings due to high upfront costs and complex maintenance. This paper presents a lower cost, prefabricated, data-driven alternative designed for home units. The structure integrates real-time environmental sensing with predictive controls to adjust shading components in response to changing environments. Simulation results show that the proposed approach outperforms static and rule-based scenarios, achieving a 12% reduction in cooling demand during summer months compared to the static baseline. This finding demonstrates the potential of accessible adaptive shading systems to mitigate heat stress while decrease cooling energy use in residential contexts. © 2026 ASHRAE.
INDEX KEYWORDS: Climate change; Predictive control systems; Cooling solutions; Data driven; Energy; Environmental conditions; Global demand; Heatwaves; Low-costs; Mechanical systems; Occupant comforts; Shading structures; Office buildings

Tekin B.H.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Tekin, Bekir Huseyin (57816594500)

From greenwashing to grounded practice: A context-specific biophilic design framework for hot arid climates
(2026) Building and Environment, 292, art. no. 114295, Cited 1 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.buildenv.2026.114295
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105028592145?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: Department of Architecture, Faculty of Arts, Design and Architecture, Sakarya University, Turkey
ABSTRACT: The accelerating impacts of climate change demand design frameworks that reduce environmental burdens while enhancing human well-being. Although biophilic design is increasingly recognised as a regenerative paradigm, its direct application in hot-arid regions remains limited and often unsuitably adapted from temperate contexts, resulting in “greenwashing” and ecological imbalance. As arid regions continue to expand, the need for climate-adaptive design in these contexts is increasingly urgent. Using an exploratory sequential mixed-methods design, this study synthesises a systematic review of six high-quality papers and in-depth interviews with five regional experts to develop a conceptual framework for biophilic design in desert environments. The findings show that temperate biophilia, largely aesthetic and visual, fails under arid conditions. The framework defines two interlinked dimensions of success: the human response (thermal comfort, well-being, and cultural resonance) and the ecological response (resource efficiency and habitat creation). It identifies passive design, water neutrality, native flora, and privacy-sensitive spatial configurations as prerequisites for restorative biophilic environments. Key strategies include hybrid ventilation, adaptive shading hierarchies, kinetic façades, reuse of traditional materials, and multi-scalar microclimates that mitigate urban heat. Acting as a complementary conceptual layer to regional sustainability standards (e.g., Al Sa'fat, GSAS, Mostadam, Estidama), the framework positions biophilic design as a climate-adaptive, ecologically grounded, and culturally embedded methodology. This work provides a conceptual foundation for policymakers and designers seeking integrated strategies that align climate mitigation with human adaptation in the rapidly expanding climatic zone. © 2026 Elsevier Ltd
AUTHOR KEYWORDS: Biophilic design; Climate adaptation; Climate mitigation; Climate-adaptive architecture; Ecological resilience; Hot-arid climate; Human well-being

Azimi M., Mahdavinejad M., Yeganeh M., Zamani Z.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Azimi, Mehdi (59972970700); Mahdavinejad, Mohammadjavad (53164158600); Yeganeh, Mansour (57204044915); Zamani, Zahra (55754005900)
59972970700; 53164158600; 57204044915; 55754005900
Real-time adaptive algae bioreactor facades: a novel method for dynamic daylight and thermal efficiency optimization in buildings
(2025) Energy Conversion and Management, 346, art. no. 120409, Cited 1 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.enconman.2025.120409
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105014520687?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: Department of Architecture, Faculty of Art and Architecture, Tarbiat Modares University, Tehran, Iran; College of Engineering and Architecture, University of Nizwa, Oman; Architectural Design, Modeling and Fabrication Lab, Department of Architecture, Tarbiat Modares University, Tehran, Iran; Department of Arch. Technology, College of Fine Art, University of Tehran, Iran
ABSTRACT: This study presents the Algae Autobioreactor (AAB), an advanced photo-bioreactor system designed for architectural integration as windows, building facades, and shading elements. We developed the Real-time Image Processing Automation of the Algae Panel (RIPAAP) system to enable continuous monitoring of critical parameters including algal concentration, density, Visible Transmittance (VT), Solar Heat Gain Coefficient (SHGC), and U-value. Unlike conventional approaches limited to static algal concentrations, our method captures dynamic growth patterns over extended periods. The dynamic growth patterns were analyzed in two distinct configurations: Case 1 (vertical slats) and Case 2 (horizontal slats), representing fundamentally different shading approaches. Experimental results demonstrate distinct behavioral patterns: in Case 1, initial concentrations of 20–30 % exhibited rapid fluctuations between 40–90 % during subsequent days before stabilizing, accompanied by reduced VT and SHGC variability. Case 2 showed similar initial behavior but maintained higher concentration volatility through day 4. Quantitative analysis revealed VT variations of 0.05–0.18 (40–80 % concentration) and SHGC ranges of 0.09–0.20 for Case 1, while Case 2 displayed VT changes of 0.02–0.17 (40–90 % concentration) with SHGC values of 0.02–0.21. The RIPAAP system's ability to track these transient phenomena in real-time represents a significant advancement over traditional measurement techniques, offering new capabilities for optimizing both Energy Efficiency (EE) and Human Health (HH) parameters in building-integrated bioreactor systems. These findings establish a foundation for dynamic control of algal-based architectural elements and demonstrate the critical relationship between concentration changes and optical/thermal performance metrics. © 2025 Elsevier Ltd

Zhang C., Zhong W., Li K., Hu M., Zheng H., Zhang T.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Zhang, Chengshen (60510530900); Zhong, Wenzhou (57225694065); Li, Ke (58759136800); Hu, Maomao (57194381793); Zheng, Hao (57209660921); Zhang, Tong (59344526500)
60510530900; 57225694065; 58759136800; 57194381793; 57209660921; 59344526500
Surrogate model-based design optimization of modular façades under multifunctional scenarios
(2026) Building and Environment, 295, art. no. 114475, Cited 2 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.buildenv.2026.114475
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105033239035?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: School of Architecture, Southeast University, 2 Sipailou, Nanjing, 210096, China; Department of the Built Environment, College of Design and Engineering, National University of Singapore, 4 Architecture Drive, 117566, Singapore; Architectural Intelligence Group, Department of Architecture and Civil Engineering, City University of Hong Kong, Hong Kong Special Administrative Region of China, China
ABSTRACT: As existing buildings are increasingly retrofitted in response to frequently changing demands, the configuration of façade systems becomes critical for ensuring adaptability to evolving functional use and climate conditions. While modular façades offer this potential, current approaches remain case-specific and simulation-intensive, constraining early-stage exploration of scenario-based design and performance-driven optimization. This study proposes an image-to-image surrogate framework that couples a generative adversarial network (GAN) with multi-objective optimization to support design optimization of modular façades in open-plan offices. Using a university educational building in Singapore as a case study, each façade opening-ratio maps (12 × 18 panels) and corresponding annual fields of daylight autonomy (DA), glare autonomy (GA), and thermal comfort percentage (TCP) are generated and encoded as 256 × 256 grayscale images for model training. The calibrated surrogate model accurately reproduces spatial performance patterns and reduces per-evaluation runtime by nearly 90% compared to simulations. Scenario experiments across three representative layouts demonstrate that surrogate-based single and multi-objective optimization decreases combined the root mean square error (RMSE) by 13.7–21.7%, while designer-specified inputs increase errors by up to 57.1% in complex layouts. The results highlight that data-driven façade configurations better capture thermo-optical trade-offs than experience-based schemes. The proposed framework offers a scalable, visually spatial decision-support tool for adaptive modular façade retrofits and can be extended to additional climates, geometries, and performance objectives. © 2026 Elsevier Ltd

Ohene E., McGehee M.D., Krarti M.

Evaluation of smart glazing properties for net positive windows in commercial buildings
(2026) Energy and Buildings, 357, art. no. 117129, Cited 0 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.enbuild.2026.117129
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105029647797?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: Department of Civil, Environmental and Architectural Engineering, United States; Department of Chemical Engineering, University of Colorado Boulder, United States
ABSTRACT: The study identifies smart glazing properties necessary for delivering net energy positive performance for office buildings. A comprehensive parametric framework is used to quantify the influence of smart window optical and thermal characteristics, tint control strategies, and building design parameters on whole-building energy performance. The analysis results demonstrate that smart glazed windows can transition from net energy liability to net energy positive elements of office building envelopes when climate-responsive control strategies and daylighting integration are appropriately implemented. Without daylighting control, smart glazed windows can increase annual energy use by up to 20% in cooling-dominated climates, whereas the deployment of daylighting control reduced total energy consumption by 5–7% in mixed climates such as Boulder, CO. Outdoor air temperature (OAT)-based control strategies consistently outperformed incident total solar-radiation (ITSR)-based controls, when coupled with optimal switching settings. Moreover, the sensitivity analyses reveal a nonlinear dependence of energy performance on window-to-wall ratio, with large windows increasing both the penalties and benefits of smart glazed fenestration systems. Improvements in thermal transmittance below 1.10 W/m2·K yielded diminishing returns, indicating that adaptive optical properties are more influential than ultra-low U-factors for achieving net energy positive performance for smart glazed windows. The evaluation of commercially available electrochromic glazing products when deployed to office buildings show energy savings of 2–5% for mild and mixed climates, near-neutral performance for cooling-dominated climates, and energy penalties for heating-dominated climates. © 2026 Elsevier B.V.

Dokhaei B., Alipour A., Abdelaziz K., Shafei B., Sarkar P., Hobeck J.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Dokhaei, Bahareh (58951249600); Alipour, Alice (56414498100); Abdelaziz, Khalid (56182251800); Shafei, Behrouz (34870505300); Sarkar, Partha (35598272200); Hobeck, Jared (55179070100)
58951249600; 56414498100; 56182251800; 34870505300; 35598272200; 55179070100
Cyber-physical smart morphing facades driven by reinforcement learning for wind-induced vibration control in tall buildings
(2026) Journal of Wind Engineering and Industrial Aerodynamics, 273, art. no. 106394, Cited 0 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.jweia.2026.106394
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105034627643?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: Department of Civil, Construction and Environmental Engineering, Iowa State University, United States; Department of Mechanical and Nuclear Engineering, Kansas State University, United States; Department of Aerospace Engineering, Iowa State University, United States
ABSTRACT: Modern tall and slender structures often experience significant wind-induced vibrations (WIV). This study explores the effectiveness of a smart morphing façade system featuring motorized vertical corner plates to control WIV in high-rise buildings. A novel cyber-physical framework is proposed and validated through wind tunnel experiments using a 1:76 scaled aeroelastic building model. The influence of surrounding buildings on wind turbulence is also considered, ensuring adaptability to dynamic urban environments. To enable real-time responsiveness and optimize plate orientations, the system integrates an encoder-decoder Gated Recurrent Unit (GRU) model and a Genetic Reinforcement Learning (GRL) algorithm. The GRU model defines the system state using data from pressure probes, accelerometers, and atmospheric sensors. At each control interval, the GRL algorithm leverages past plate angles and GRU states to train multi-layer perceptron (MLP) policies. It combines genetic algorithms (GAs) with random search to balance exploration and exploitation while optimizing future plate angles. The results show significant reductions in root mean square (RMS) accelerations, up to 50% compared to the uncontrolled model. Frequency domain analysis also reveals a decrease in energy around dominant frequencies, confirming the system's effectiveness. This represents a pioneering advancement in the development of smart morphing facade systems that adapt to wind conditions and mitigate structural vibrations efficiently. © 2026

Zhao H., Li S., Wu Y., Sun H., Lin B.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Zhao, Hengxin (57222094200); Li, Shiying (59757384400); Wu, Yifan (57212210945); Sun, Hongli (57189907547); Lin, Borong (7403508277)
57222094200; 59757384400; 57212210945; 57189907547; 7403508277
Improved superhydrophobic surface for new phase-change thermal diode in building thermal management
(2026) Energy, 347, art. no. 140161, Cited 0 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.energy.2026.140161
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105029407767?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: Department of Building Science, Tsinghua University, Beijing, 100084, China; Key Laboratory of Eco Planning & Green Building, Ministry of Education, Tsinghua University, China; College of Architecture and Environment, Sichuan University, Chengdu, 610065, China
ABSTRACT: Compared with traditional dynamic building envelopes, the performance-adjustable building envelopes combined with the new phase-change thermal diode offer the advantages of excellent adjustability and straightforward control. To further enhance the unidirectional heat transfer capability and durability of the new phase-change thermal diode in building applications, this study employed chemical etching to fabricate a silver nitrate-based superhydrophobic surface with small structural spacing. The experimental results show that the application of a silver nitrate-based superhydrophobic surface effectively ensured the durability and stability of the new phase-change thermal diode. The maximum forward thermal conductivity was found to be 8.85 W/(m·K) and the thermal rectification reached 78.81. This represents an improvement of between 30.89 % and 249.53 % compared to the laser-fabricated superhydrophobic surface. Additionally, simulation demonstrated the energy-saving potential of dynamic building envelopes combined with the new phase-change thermal diode in data centers across different climate zones. The maximum annual energy savings reached 325.62 kWh/m2 were achieved, demonstrating significant application value. This study promotes the transition of performance-adjustable building envelopes from theoretical research to practical application, thereby fostering the dual development of the buildings and materials fields. © 2026

Pantilimonescu-Cioancă F., Alibrandi U., Beatini V.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Pantilimonescu-Cioancă, Florina (60260381200); Alibrandi, Umberto (23666588200); Beatini, Valentina (55753420700)

Predicting emotional impact of adaptive façades through Machine Learning, A VR-based study with PAD assessment
(2026) Building and Environment, 297, art. no. 114541, Cited 0 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.buildenv.2026.114541
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105034360495?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: Ion Mincu University of Architecture and Urbanism, Bucharest, 010014, Romania; Umberto Alibrandi, National University of Singapore, Department of Civil and Environmental Engineering; Aarhus University, Dept Civil and Architectural Engineering, Aarhus, 8000, Denmark
ABSTRACT: As global mental health concerns rise and indoor environments dominate daily life, understanding how architecture shapes emotional well-being has become urgent. Adaptive façades hold promise for creating responsive environments, yet their emotional impact remains poorly understood due to complex relationships between façade parameters and subjective responses. This study establishes a Machine Learning framework to assess architecture's emotional impact through adaptive shading effects. Using a Virtual Reality experiment, the Pleasure, Arousal, and Dominance (PAD) emotional responses were collected from 111 participants exposed to dynamic façade configurations, varying parameters such as speed, spatial spread, and coverage ratio. Challenges of subjective data were addressed through consensus-based classification and probabilistic modelling, evaluating multiple algorithms based on KL Divergence and Ranked Probability Score. Random Forest emerged optimal, achieving 85% accuracy for pleasure prediction in consensus analysis and 75% top-k accuracy in probabilistic modelling. Participant clustering improved performance to 87% for pleasure, while arousal proved most challenging to predict. Shapley Additive Explanations revealed spread, speed, and coverage ratio as most influential parameters. Optimal design zones occur at moderate speeds (<0.1 m/s) with medium spread ratios (0-10. Such configurations can describe low-distraction dynamic louvers or blinds, supporting calm atmosphere in common working, learning or relaxing spaces. The findings provide transferable, implementation-agnostic, perceptual design principles and parameter thresholds for dynamic shading in adaptive façades. By embracing uncertainty rather than averaging it out, the probabilistic framework offers adaptive systems that respect user diversity. Results advance integration of emotional considerations into evidence-based architectural practice. © 2026 Elsevier Ltd

Yousaf A., Koç M.

Bio-inspired 4D printing of earthen soil composites for structural living wall applications
(2026) Sustainable Materials and Technologies, 48, art. no. e01957, Cited 0 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.susmat.2026.e01957
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105032991932?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: Division of Sustainable Development, College of Science and Engineering, Hamad bin Khalifa University, Doha, Qatar
ABSTRACT: Rising carbon emissions highlight the need for multifunctional building materials that move beyond inert structural roles toward ecological performance. Vertical greening and living wall systems (LWS) offer climate-responsive façades, yet most rely on synthetic substrates with high embodied carbon, limited biodegradability, and poor long-term moisture retention. This study introduces a 4D printing (4DP) approach for continuous living wall systems, using time as the transformative dimension as printed components evolve biologically. Bio-based earthen composites composed of local Qatari soil, acacia gum, and nanoclay were extrusion-printed to fabricate structural panels capable of supporting germination after printing. 9 formulations were prepared and assessed for rheology, extrudability, buildability, mechanical strength, thermal stability, shrinkage, and germination performance. Germination tests revealed a clear structural–biological relationship: low-binder mixes displayed high shrinkage and rapid moisture loss that inhibited sprouting, while higher acacia-gum dosages improved cohesion, internal moisture retention, and microbial compatibility. Nanoclay enhanced thixotropy, minimized shrinkage, and improved filament stability. The optimal formulation, T3 (90 wt% soil, 6 wt% acacia gum, 0.6 wt% nanoclay), provided stable extrusion, buildability up to 75 layers, and compressive strength above 4 MPa at 28 days. Panels printed using T3 demonstrated rapid biological activation; under controlled humidity, seeds germinated within 54 h while the printed geometry remained dimensionally stable. These results demonstrate that extrusion-based, bio-stabilized earthen composites can act as multifunctional materials that integrate structural integrity, moisture regulation, and biological receptivity. The work establishes a pathway structural element that function as true 4D living structures, evolving over time through controlled germination. Copyright © 2026. Published by Elsevier B.V.

Aalami N., Abravesh M., Maqsood T., Bhuiyan M.A.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Aalami, Negin (59166987500); Abravesh, Mahdieh (57209541345); Maqsood, Tariq (57221378564); Bhuiyan, Muhammed A. (57203027631)

Movement optimization of a movable façade using bimetal as actuators for enhanced indoor airflow
(2026) Energy and Buildings, 351, art. no. 116753, Cited 1 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.enbuild.2025.116753
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105023149306?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: School of Architecture, College of Fine Arts, University of Tehran, Tehran, 1415564583, Iran; STEM College, School of Engineering, RMIT University, VIC, Melbourne, 3001, Australia
ABSTRACT: Contemporary building design increasingly incorporates adaptive envelope systems, such as external louvered blinds, to respond to environmental stimuli and enhance thermal comfort while managing indoor airflow. This study investigates the use of bimetallic materials as passive actuators, integrated with aluminium substrates, to drive thermally responsive louver movement. Three design parameters are examined: bimetal coating percentages, bimetal layers, and aluminium base thickness. Twelve material configurations were evaluated using nonlinear thermo-mechanical finite element analysis, with results validated by laboratory experiments. It demonstrated that solar-induced heating raised the bimetal-integrated aluminium surface temperature by approximately 1–15 °C above ambient, producing free-end flap displacements consistent with Finite Element Method (FEM) predictions. The influence of louver movement on indoor air velocity was analyzed using coupled Computational Fluid Dynamics (CFD) simulations across five coastal Australian cities with diverse climatic conditions. The results demonstrated that reducing the aluminium plate thickness and applying at least 70 % bimetal coating increased flap displacement, exceeding 3 mm, reaching up to 5 mm. Elevated ambient temperatures led to larger deflections and enhanced indoor air velocities, improving passive ventilation performance in warmer climates. This research offers valuable design guidance for optimizing adaptive louver systems to improve natural ventilation and reduce energy consumption in residential buildings. © 2025 Elsevier B.V.

AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Cai, Guoshun (56383381400); Yin, Guodong (24781523700); Wang, Jinxiang (55969148200); Feng, Jiwei (57202678087); Bai, Shuo (57577884700); Hu, Jingyu (57221155591)
56383381400; 24781523700; 55969148200; 57202678087; 57577884700; 57221155591
Observer-based robust MPC for trajectory tracking of autonomous vehicles with dynamic envelopes
(2026) Control Engineering Practice, 172, art. no. 106893, Cited 1 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.conengprac.2026.106893
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105032881431?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: School of Mechanical Engineering, Southeast University, Jiangsu, Nanjing, 211189, China; School of Automotive Engineering, Liaocheng University, Shandong, Liaocheng, 252000, China; School of Cyber Science and Engineering, Southeast University, Jiangsu, Nanjing, 211189, China
ABSTRACT: Trajectory tracking of autonomous vehicles in open-road environments is inherently subject to unpredictable uncertainties, external disturbances, and nonholonomic system constraints. To rigorously address these challenges, this paper proposes an observer-based robust model predictive control (MPC) with theoretically guaranteed robustness and tracking performance. First, a linear parameter-varying (LPV) system representation is formulated by embedding the time-varying tire cornering stiffness and longitudinal velocity into a unified polytopic structure. Then, to enable real-time estimation of the vehicle sideslip angle, a quadratic-boundedness-based robust observer with offline gain computation is designed. The estimation error set is updated online by computing the minimal admissible bound on the observation error. Next, the observer-based robust min-max MPC problem is systematically formulated via constructing tractable linear matrix inequalities (LMIs). Closed-loop stability is guaranteed by incorporating the terminal invariant set. Phase-plane-based vehicle stability region and control barrier function (CBF)-based safety constraints are integrated into the robust MPC framework in a manner that rigorously preserves Lyapunov stability. Subsequently, an offline solution is devised to alleviate the online computational burden and facilitate real-time deployment. Finally, hardware-in-the-loop (HIL) experiments and real-vehicle tests with recent benchmark methods validate the effectiveness and superiority of the proposed approach. © 2026 Elsevier Ltd

Chen G., Zhang G., Liu Y., Li R., Rong L.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Chen, Guoxing (57214931586); Zhang, Guoqiang (55749716300); Liu, Yanni (36118932800); Li, Riyi (57202788253); Rong, Li (57190397380)
57214931586; 55749716300; 36118932800; 57202788253; 57190397380
Impacts of balconies on air quality within idealized building array: CFD modelling with solar radiation
(2026) Building and Environment, 298, art. no. 114615, Cited 0 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.buildenv.2026.114615
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105036251755?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: College of Engineering, South China Agricultural University, Guangzhou, 510642, China; Department of Civil and Architectural Engineering, Aarhus University, Inge Lehmanns Gade 10, Aarhus C, DK-8000, Denmark; School of Civil Engineering and Transportation, Guangzhou University, Guangzhou, 510006, China; State Key Laboratory of Subtropical Building and Urban Science, School of Architecture, South China University of Technology, Guangzhou, 510640, China
ABSTRACT: The impact of balconies on outdoor microclimate has typically been overlooked in previous studies. This study investigated how balconies affect airflow and pollutant dispersion within an idealized street canyon using computational fluid dynamics (CFD) with fully coupled radiative, convective, and conductive heat transfer processes. Steady-state CFD simulations were conducted using weather data from a typical day in June in Guangzhou, China, at four representative times of the day (0800, 1200, 1600 and 2000 Local Solar Time (LST)). The results showed that outdoor air quality was influenced by time of the day, balcony form and location. The best and worst outdoor air quality occurred at 0800 LST and 2000 LST, respectively, and pollutant concentrations at pedestrian zone overwhelmingly exceeded those at vertically distributed balcony zones. When only the near-façade pedestrian zone was evaluated, the presence of balconies worsened the outdoor air quality significantly. When all the balcony zones were evaluated, the presence of balconies worsened the outdoor air quality at 0800 LST but improved it at 1200 LST. Generally, the All-sealed balcony is most effective in promoting better air quality in the near-façade areas. Pronounced thermal stratifications on building surfaces developed not only during periods of solar radiation but also at night, driven by temperature differences between interior and exterior surfaces. Overall, the findings highlight the importance of accurately representing façade geometry and dynamic thermal boundary conditions in CFD studies of urban microclimate. Future work should investigate how aspect ratio, balcony configuration, and building materials jointly influence outdoor microclimate. © 2026 Elsevier Ltd

Miraba S., Salehi A., Rostamzadeh-Renani M., Ehteshami R., Shahbazi A., Rostamzadeh-Renani R., Dehkordi S.A.H.H., Baghoolizadeh M.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Miraba, Sepideh (57221044767); Salehi, Ali (57211500259); Rostamzadeh-Renani, Mohammad (57216954326); Ehteshami, Reza (59353491700); Shahbazi, Armin (60133919100); Rostamzadeh-Renani, Reza (57216950040); Dehkordi, Seyed Amir Hossein Hashemi (57824576300); Baghoolizadeh, Mohammadreza (57338920800)
57221044767; 57211500259; 57216954326; 59353491700; 60133919100; 57216950040; 57824576300; 57338920800
Adaptive BIPV shading optimization for electricity generation and building electricity management, sDA, and DGP using multi-objective algorithms
(2025) Applied Energy, 402, art. no. 126860, Cited 11 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.apenergy.2025.126860
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105018307628?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: University of Cincinnati, The College of Design, Architecture, Art and Planning, School of Planning, Cincinnati, United States; Thomas Jefferson University, College of Architecture and The Built Environment, East Falls Campus, Philadelphia, PA, United States; Energy Department, Politecnico di Milano, Via Lambruschini 4, Milan, 20156, Italy; Department of Mechanical Engineering, Shahrekord University, Shahrekord, 88186-34141, Iran
ABSTRACT: Background: Adaptive Building-Integrated Photovoltaic Shading Systems (BIPVS) offer a promising solution to simultaneously address sustainable energy generation, daylight optimization, and visual comfort in office buildings. However, current studies mostly focus on static or semi-static shading solutions with limited adaptability to real-time solar conditions. Objective: This study proposes a fully adaptive BIPVS shading configuration and evaluates its performance through comprehensive multi-objective optimization, targeting maximum electricity production (EP), minimum electricity consumption (EC), improved spatial daylight autonomy (sDA), and reduced daylight glare probability (DGP) in an office building located in Tehran, Iran. Methodology: EnergyPlus, Radiance, and Python scripting were integrated into a novel simulation-optimization framework. Four advanced multi-objective algorithms (Multi-Objective Grey Wolf Optimizer Algorithm (MOGWO), Multi-Objective Whale Optimization Algorithm (MOWOA), Multi-Objective Ant Colony Optimization Algorithm (MOACO), Multi-Objective Moth Flame Optimization Algorithm (MOMFO)) were applied to optimize design variables, including tilt angle, azimuth angle, shading-to-window distance, window dimensions, and window-to-wall ratio. Performance was evaluated using eight criteria (Generational distance (GD), Inverted generational distance (IGD), Spacing (SP), Maximum Spread (MS), Time (T), Quality (Q), Mean ideal distance (MID), and the number of Pareto front points (NPS)), ranked by Shannon entropy, and the best solution identified by the TOPSIS method. Results: The findings show that adaptive solar shading can boost net electricity generation by over 200 %, achieving up to 6661.11 kWh annually, while reducing building energy consumption by 22.29 % and improving daylight autonomy by 80 %. Additionally, glare probability was significantly reduced, enhancing occupant visual comfort. This research highlights the potential of adaptive BIPVS systems, combined with advanced optimization methods, to significantly improve building energy efficiency and indoor environmental quality, particularly in semi-arid climates. © 2025 Elsevier Ltd

Yao Z., Lu W., Zhang H., Wang X., Zhu L., Shi Y.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Yao, Zhuonan (59194182500); Lu, Wantong (60228512500); Zhang, Hui (59291389200); Wang, Xinyu (56086681300); Zhu, Lihua (60613233100); Shi, Yan (55495668200)
59194182500; 60228512500; 59291389200; 56086681300; 60613233100; 55495668200
Spatiotemporal dynamics of thermal comfort, energy consumption, and carbon emissions in climate-adaptive MPCMs used for building envelopes across 11 cities with continental climate
(2026) Energy, 356, art. no. 141160, Cited 0 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.energy.2026.141160
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105037745526?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: School of Biotechnology and Food Science, Tianjin University of Commerce, Tianjin, 300134, China; School of Mechanical Engineering, Tianjin University of Commerce, Tianjin, 300134, China; School of Foreign Languages, Tianjin University of Commerce, Tianjin, 300134, China
ABSTRACT: To achieve the synergistic optimization of thermal comfort, energy consumption, and carbon emissions, the microencapsulated phase change materials (MPCMs)-reinforced fast-growing Eucommia ulmoides (EU) fibers concrete composite walls (MPCMs-EUWs) were designed and evaluated in 11 representative cities across the Dfa/Dwb and Dfb/Dwb climate zones. The year-round thermal comfort and sustainability simulations were performed. The effects of MPCMs with varying phase change temperatures (18 °C, 24 °C, 28 °C, 32 °C, and 37 °C) on the Predicted Mean Vote (PMV), total operational energy consumption, and carbon emissions were evaluated. Simulation results showed that, among MPCMs with varying phase change temperatures, PMV was effectively modulated by the optimal composite strategies. For the cities of Chicago, Beijing, Seoul, Almaty, Ottawa, Moscow, Helsinki, Calgary, Vladivostok and Warsaw, the comfortable PMV range of −1.1–0.0 was achieved by MPCMs18-EUWs, and the range of 0.1–+1.9 was achieved by MPCMs32-EUWs/MPCMs37-EUWs. Furthermore, substantial reductions in the minimum monthly operational energy consumption (15.37–27.19 MJ/m2) and carbon emissions (0.37–4.65 kgCO2/m2) were achieved in these 10 cities. Although this primary strategy was effective for most cities, region-specific tailored composite strategy of MPCMs28-EUWs, MPCMs24-EUWs, and MPCMs37-EUWs was identified for Bucharest. In addition, a novel energy-PMV synergy index is first proposed to quantify the synergistic effect of thermal comfort and total operational energy consumption. In summary, this work provides a strategy for selecting thermal comfort, energy-efficient, and low-carbon building materials in 11 representative cities. © 2026 Elsevier Ltd. All rights are reserved, including those for text and data mining, AI training, and similar technologies.

Shi S., Zhu N., Jiang Y., Dong G., Lin B.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Shi, Shaohang (57396234600); Zhu, Ning (57212916460); Jiang, Yong (60645568800); Dong, Guoqun (60644988800); Lin, Borong (7403508277)
57396234600; 57212916460; 60645568800; 60644988800; 7403508277
Optimization of thermally variable solar collection walls integrated with flat plate gravity heat pipes
(2026) Energy and Buildings, 365, art. no. 117660, Cited 0 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.enbuild.2026.117660
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105039813460?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: Department of Building Science, Tsinghua University, Beijing, 100084, China; Key Laboratory of Eco Planning & Green Building, Ministry of Education, Tsinghua University, Beijing, 100084, China; CSCEC JIUHE Development Group Co. Ltd., Beijing, 100070, China
ABSTRACT: Extreme climates heighten the need for climate adaptation in low-carbon buildings. Taking thermally variable solar collection walls with flat plate gravity heat pipes as an example, the study explores the effects of geometric parameters and the structure’s radiative characteristics on building energy efficiency. Results showed that increasing the thickness can increase the wall’s heat transfer capacity by 45.0 W/m2 (corresponding to a 36.0%). Still, the increase in the effective heat transfer length shows a negative correlation, and the evaporation section may even decrease by 52.4 W (15.8% of the total). Heat collection performance varied by orientation and season, with the west façade accumulating the most heat in summer and the south façade in winter. Optimizing the evaporation section’s emissivity can increase annual heat collection by 132.57 kW·h (19.49% increase). Electrochromic cover glazing can decrease the annual heat collection by 147.29–994.12 kW·h. Based on the cross-region comparison, the winter heating energy-saving rates in Lhasa, Beijing, and Harbin are 73.08%, 6.42%, and 1.23%, and increases in summer cooling energy consumption are 8.23%, 1.01%, and 2.20%, respectively. Findings can support the development of climate-adaptive building envelopes. Copyright © 2026. Published by Elsevier B.V.

Huang S., Zhang D., Wang J., Xiao Y., Shang C., Wang F., Yin S.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Huang, Shan (60239940800); Zhang, Duo (60645534000); Wang, Junsong (57112783500); Xiao, Yiqiang (42862811300); Shang, Chuyu (60645626900); Wang, Fengyin (60239841200); Yin, Shi (55872035500)
60239940800; 60645534000; 57112783500; 42862811300; 60645626900; 60239841200; 55872035500
Wind-driven rain mitigation effects of building facade shading systems: insights from comparative field measurements
(2026) Building and Environment, 301, art. no. 114752, Cited 0 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.buildenv.2026.114752
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105039786699?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: South China University of Technology School of Architecture, China
ABSTRACT: Wind-driven rain (WDR) represents a notable concern for building envelopes and indoor environmental quality in hot and humid regions. Although field measurements have characterized WDR distribution patterns on facades, the role of building facade shading systems in WDR mitigation remains underexplored. Building on our previous finding that the buffer zone formed by overhangs can effectively mitigate WDR, this study investigated the modulation mechanisms and mitigation effects of a shading system comprising overhangs and vertical louvers. Synchronized high-resolution wind and rain data were collected via field measurements to compare the shading system with both the directly exposed zone and the buffer zone. Results show that the shading system substantially altered the near-wall flow field, reduced the wind speed inside the shading system by 74.4%–83.9% and shifted the three-dimensional turbulence intensity ratio from 1:0.7:0.4 to 1:0.8:0.6. Raindrop behavior suggests size sorting and breakup: medium raindrops (0.937–2.215 mm) exhibited the highest interception efficiency; small raindrops (<0.562 mm) were deflected by the airflow; large raindrops (>3.75 mm) penetrated due to inertial momentum. Wind speed and rainfall intensity exhibited a significant negative synergy on the WDR mitigation rate. Quantitative analysis shows that the shading system achieves overall WDR mitigation rates of 80% relative to the directly exposed zone and 40% relative to the buffer zone, with the effect strongly dependent on raindrop diameter. This study enhances both empirical and theoretical understanding of WDR mitigation by shading systems, providing a scientific foundation for climate-adaptive facade design. © 2026 Elsevier Ltd

Chen Q., Mao H., Hu W., Pan L.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Chen, Qianhong (60637266700); Mao, Huijun (57221619043); Hu, Wentao (57189218772); Pan, Lan (36492197000)

Green facades and building energy performance: a multi-climate perspective
(2026) Building and Environment, 300, art. no. 114744, Cited 0 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.buildenv.2026.114744
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105038814362?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: College of Forestry and Landscape Architecture, South China Agricultural University, Guangzhou, 510642, China; School of Architecture, State Key Laboratory of Subtropical Building and Urban Science, South China University of Technology, Guangzhou, 510640, China
ABSTRACT: Green facades (GFs) offer a promising strategy for improving building energy performance and indoor thermal comfort; however, their effectiveness is highly climate-dependent. This study presents a systematic cross-climate evaluation of direct green facades (DGFs) and indirect green facades (IGFs) across seven Köppen–Geiger climate zones using an EMS-integrated dynamic heat-transfer (DHT) model coupled with EnergyPlus simulations. Results show that DGFs generally achieve greater reductions in interior surface temperature, with a maximum decrease of 2.9 °C observed in the hot desert (Bwh) climate. In contrast, IGFs are more effective in reducing inward heat flux, reaching a peak reduction of 29.2 W/m² in Bwh due to the additional thermal resistance provided by the air cavity. Cooling-load reductions are most significant in hot and high-radiation climates, where IGFs achieve the highest annual cooling-load saving rate of 13.12 % in Bwh, while DGFs outperform IGFs in the remaining climate zones. Heating-load reductions are generally more pronounced for IGFs in climates with non-negligible heating demand, such as Cfb, due to their enhanced insulation effect. Regarding indoor thermal comfort, both GF types reduce mean radiant temperature (Tmrt), with the largest improvement observed in the temperate oceanic climate (Cfb), where DGFs achieve a maximum reduction of 1.3 °C. Overall, the results demonstrate that GF performance is governed by the interplay between climatic conditions, facade configuration, and envelope thermal properties. These findings highlight the importance of climate-adaptive selection of green facade systems to maximize energy savings and thermal comfort benefits. © 2026 Elsevier Ltd

Zhao J., Fan Y., Lu J., Luo X., Ge J.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Zhao, Jiahong (57807911000); Fan, Yifan (57190574353); Lu, Jiang (55701432000); Luo, Xiaoyu (23018998900); Ge, Jian (48861340600)
57807911000; 57190574353; 55701432000; 23018998900; 48861340600
Evaluating permeable pavements' impact on the thermal environment in street canyons through outdoor experiments under hot and humid background climate
(2026) Building and Environment, 288, art. no. 113948, Cited 3 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.buildenv.2025.113948
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105021472577?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: College of Civil Engineering and Architecture, Zhejiang University, Hangzhou, 310058, China; Center of Balance Architecture, Zhejiang University, Hangzhou, 310028, China; Architectural Design and Research Institute of Zhejiang University Co., Ltd, Hangzhou, 310028, China; International Research Center for Green Building and Low-Carbon City, International Campus, Zhejiang University, Haining, 314400, China; School of Civil Engineering and Architecture, Zhejiang University of Science and Technology, Hangzhou, 310023, China
ABSTRACT: Permeable pavements are widely adopted in urban areas for their hydrological benefits and ability to mitigate the urban heat island (UHI) effect through evaporative cooling. However, dynamic shading by buildings significantly alters the surface energy balance, and their cooling efficacy remains insufficiently validated. This study presents a comparative analysis of ceramic permeable bricks (PB) and impervious concrete (IC) under dry and wet conditions across seasons, based on controlled experiments in street canyons and open areas. Results indicate that in a street canyon with an aspect ratio (H/W) of 1 and a northeast–southwest orientation, the near-surface radiation reception efficiency (the ratio of near-surface to background daily global radiation) decreased by 31%, reducing PB's sensible heat fraction from 41% to 23%. Although PB shows a higher midday surface sensible heat flux by 17 W/m2, the daily integrated flux is reduced by 0.08 kWh/m2. During the day, the PB in the canyon is 2.1 °C warmer than IC at noon, but cools at night, being 2.0 °C lower at midnight due to its lower albedo and thermal diffusivity. Moreover, the semi-enclosed geometry of the street canyon helps extend the vertical reach of the cooling and humidifying effects to over 0.5 m, whereas in open areas this effect is below 0.3 m. PB can also improve human thermal comfort by reducing the duration of extreme heat stress by 11.1%.These findings demonstrate the impact of permeable pavements on urban thermal environments, providing evidence for thermal optimization and reference data for simulations, informing climate-adaptive urban planning. © 2025
AUTHOR KEYWORDS: Outdoor experiment; Permeable pavements; Street canyon; Thermal environment

Wang C., Ji J., Wang W., Ho T.C., He W., Tso C.Y.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Wang, Chuyao (57219539826); Ji, Jie (57205430063); Wang, Wenqi (57202470701); Ho, Tsz Chung (57218372631); He, Wei (57204699316); Tso, Chi Yan (55249604200)

Adaptive photonic films for fenestration-integrated PV technology to enhance energy saving and thermal comfort: Concept and theoretical investigation
(2026) Energy Conversion and Management, 355, art. no. 121306, Cited 0 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.enconman.2026.121306
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105031735170?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: School of Energy and Environment, City University of Hong Kong, Hong Kong; Department of Thermal Science and Energy Engineering, University of Science and Technology of China, Hefei, China; Department of Building Environment and Equipment, Hefei University of Technology, Hefei, China
ABSTRACT: Photovoltaic (PV) fenestration is a widely adopted building-integrated PV technology. However, its application often results in indoor overheating during summer daytime and overcooling during winter nighttime. Traditional thermal insulation solutions are expensive to retrofit and fail to harness the potential energy benefits from the sky. To address these challenges, this study proposes and numerically investigates adaptive glass films based on VO2 photonic coatings for retrofitting existing PV fenestrations. The microstructure of the coating is designed and optimized using the transfer matrix method coupled with the effective medium model. The optimization results suggest that the cooling film has emissivity of about 0.44 during daytime and 0.86 at night, while the heating film has emissivity of about 0.68 during daytime and 0.26 at night. The thermal performance of PV fenestrations equipped with the optimized films is evaluated in a region with hot summers and cold winters. The simulation results indicate that the time percentage within the thermal comfort range could increase by approximately 0.6–2.5% in summer and 0.9–35.2% in winter, depending on the PV coverage ratio and tilt angle. In buildings with temperature and thermal comfort controls, the photonic coatings per unit area are predicted to yield annual electricity savings in the range of approximately 36.5–53.5 kWh and 45.1–63.0 kWh, respectively. This study proposes a conceptual solution to enhance the thermal performance of buildings integrating PV technology and provides simulation-based projections of its potential energy and comfort benefits. © 2026 Elsevier Ltd.

Hao L., Pang L., Scartezzini J.-L., Xu J., Wu Y.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Hao, Luoxi (57102572800); Pang, Lei (60224062900); Scartezzini, Jean-Louis (55906564900); Xu, Junli (58180369400); Wu, Yujie (57195836421)
57102572800; 60224062900; 55906564900; 58180369400; 57195836421
Learning personalized visual comfort: A data-driven adaptive shading control framework using artificial intelligence
(2026) Energy and Buildings, 352, art. no. 116801, Cited 0 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.enbuild.2025.116801
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105023821177?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: College of Architecture and Urban Planning, Tongji University, 1239 Siping Road, Shanghai, 200092, China; Solar Energy and Building Physics Laboratory (LESO-PB), École polytechnique fédérale de Lausanne (EPFL), Lausanne, CH-1015, Switzerland; School of Architecture, Soochow University, 199 Renai Road, Suzhou, 215031, China; CTG Shanghai Branch (CTGSH), China Three Gorges Investment Management Co., Ltd. (CTGIM), 231 Shibocun Road, Shanghai, 200003, China
ABSTRACT: Personalized shading control is critical for optimizing occupant visual comfort and building energy efficiency, yet conventional systems rely on static and generalized settings. This study proposes a machine learning (ML) framework that infers individual visual discomfort thresholds from occupants’ real-time interactions with smart blinds. A comparative evaluation of multiple models, including random forest (RF) and long short-term memory (LSTM), found that the convolutional neural networks (CNN) were most effective. Trained on a comprehensive dataset simulating 4860 unique occupant profiles, the CNN leverages a spatiotemporal reconstruction of multi-day behavioral data. In simulations, the CNN-based control system reduced behavioral mismatch scores by approximately 70 % and increased the occupant acceptability rate from 68 % to 83 % compared to a conventional default strategy. The framework’s real-world applicability was confirmed in a preliminary on-site test, where the model successfully learned a user’s preferences from a short observation period and significantly improved system performance. The findings show that model accuracy is highest for metrics that most frequently trigger overrides, such as Daylight Glare Probability (DGP), demonstrating the system’s ability to identify and respond to the most critical comfort drivers. This study presents a robust simulation-based methodology for developing occupant-centric shading systems, which shows promising results in preliminary on-site testing and has the potential to enhance indoor environmental quality and support more energy-efficient building operations. © 2025 Elsevier B.V.

Huang S., Xiao Y., Lu Y., Zhan Q., Wang F., Yang S., Yin S.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Huang, Shan (60239940800); Xiao, Yiqiang (42862811300); Lu, Yao (57209874915); Zhan, Qiaosheng (57220874432); Wang, Fengyin (60239841200); Yang, Shangjie (58636724800); Yin, Shi (55872035500)
60239940800; 42862811300; 57209874915; 57220874432; 60239841200; 58636724800; 55872035500
Mitigation effects of building facade buffer zones on wind-driven rain: Insights from field measurements
(2026) Building and Environment, 289, art. no. 114051, Cited 1 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.buildenv.2025.114051
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105024887111?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: State Key Laboratory of Subtropical Building and Urban Science, South China University of Technology, Guangzhou, 510640, China; School of Architecture, South China University of Technology, Guangzhou, 510640, China; School of Architecture and Urban Planning, Guangdong University of Technology, Guangzhou, 510000, China
ABSTRACT: Climate change has recently intensified extreme wind-rain events in China's hot-humid regions, where wind-driven rain (WDR) critically impacts building envelope durability. While research investigating the effects of WDR on building surfaces is extensive, the influence of facade design morphology remains underexplored. These regions widely employ climate-adaptive facade buffer zones exhibiting strong WDR mitigation potential. However, quantitative studies on wind-rain distribution and mitigation effects within such zones remain limited, thus further investigation is required to clarify WDR mitigation mechanisms. In this study, a sixth-floor buffer zone unit within a multistory building in Guangzhou was examined. Wind-rain parameters were measured in exterior and buffer zones, yielding high-resolution datasets during three short-duration rainfall events in 2025. The results indicated that the buffer zone reduced the mean wind speed (WS) by 65%–81% and altered the turbulence intensity ratio from 1.0:0.9:0.4 (Iu: Iv: Iw) to 1.0:0.7:0.6. Rain intensity (RI) mitigation rate decreased from 83.7% to 71.0% as RI increased. Small raindrops (<0.562 mm) accumulated because of airflow disturbance, whereas larger raindrops exhibited unimodal retention. The gamma distribution fit midrange raindrops (0.562–2.375 mm) well but deviated at the extremes. Critical thresholds for WDR mitigation efficiency (η) were identified at WS <7.05 m/s or RI <7.04 mm/h, with a synergistic suppression effect observed between WS and RI (R² = 0.806; RMSE = 0.11). The findings demonstrate that η depends on WS, RI, and raindrop size distribution (RSD), offering essential theoretical and practical support for designing WDR-resistant and climate-adaptive buildings under extreme weathers. © 2025 Elsevier Ltd

Aeinfar S., Serteser N.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Aeinfar, Sanam (59716905800); Serteser, Nuri (57202852880)

Performance optimization of parametric hexagonal façade openings for natural ventilation and cooling load reduction in high-rise buildings
(2026) Energy and Buildings, 355, art. no. 117021, Cited 0 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.enbuild.2026.117021
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105028922736?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: Department of Architecture, Construction Science, Faculty of Architecture, Istanbul Technical University, Istanbul, Turkey
ABSTRACT: This study explores the potential of parametric façade openings to enhance natural ventilation and reduce cooling loads in high-rise office buildings. These buildings often experience stagnation zones on the windward façade, typically at mid-height, where airflow divides upward and downward. Focusing on the floor intersecting the stagnation point, the study investigates how aperture design and spatial distribution can optimize airflow and thermal comfort during warm seasons. A multi-stage methodology was employed, combining CFD analysis (ANSYS Fluent) to identify façade pressure zones, with seasonal energy simulations conducted in EnergyPlus through the Honeybee interface, and generative parametric modeling in Grasshopper. Various hexagonal aperture configurations were assessed based on air change rates (ACH), operative temperature, and cooling energy use intensity (EUI). The results indicate that aperture distribution has a notable influence on ventilation performance and indoor comfort. Among the four tested configurations, the edge-to-center (EC) scenario, which represents a gradient distribution where the size and porosity of openings increase from the façade edges toward the center, achieved the highest comfort percentage and a notable reduction in cooling EUI compared to the least effective scenario. These findings demonstrate that strategic, static aperture design can effectively balance passive airflow and thermal comfort without relying on complex dynamic systems, offering a scalable solution for climate-responsive façade strategies in high-rise buildings. © 2026 Elsevier B.V.

Li Y., Song Y., Wang Z., Chen X., Song J.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Li, Yiwei (60478917400); Song, Yajin (59000187200); Wang, Zhuo (57444397800); Chen, Xuejian (58292530000); Song, Jianrong (60479589300)
60478917400; 59000187200; 57444397800; 58292530000; 60479589300
Mimosa pudica-inspired phase change-driven reversible building facades for zero-energy thermal management
(2026) Journal of Building Engineering, 123, art. no. 115816, Cited 0 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.jobe.2026.115816
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105032533589?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: School of Materials Science and Engineering, Chang'an University, Xi'an, 710061, China; State Key Laboratory of Advanced Waterproof Materials, Beijing Oriental Yuhong Waterproof Technology Co., Ltd., Beijing, 101111, China
ABSTRACT: Daily heating and cooling requirements generate approximately 50% of the global total energy demand for buildings. The exploration of active thermal management for buildings that dynamically responding to variable environments including seasonal changes, daily time variations, and solar irradiation with low energy consumption is of high importance. Inspired by the dynamic deformation responses of Mimosa pudica to light, we present kinetic reversible building facades with stacked anisotropic thermal conductivity and radiation-regulation layers that facilitate radiative heating and cooling. In response to ambient solar radiation, the deformation of the facades via a “cell-like-hinge” based on liquid–vapor phase changing achieves intelligent switching between solar heating and radiative cooling. Compared with present adaptive facades based on programing materials, the working temperature and response of our facades can be modulated simply in a wide range by grayscale of hinge with double self-weight load-bearing capacities to drive thermal regulation. The device achieves ∼40.80 W m−2 of average cooling power in hot and ∼531.4 W m−2 of average heating power in cold without any external energy consumption during the whole process. Coupled with heat-flow adjustment in accordance with the anisotropic thermal conductivity, a temperature reduction of 5 °C at an ambient temperature of 33 °C was achieved in experimental scale. Building energy simulation predicts quantitatively our prototype of dual-mode reversible building facades represents efficient radiative thermal-management strategy toward applying in a scalable and cost-efficient buildings with dynamic daily and seasonal variations. © 2026 Elsevier Ltd

Naboni E., Marchesini G.E., Grifoni R.C.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Naboni, Emanuele (55925140600); Marchesini, Graziano Enzo (60572322400); Grifoni, Roberta Cocci (55746612200)

3D façade geometry as a dual passive control in hot urban canyons: reducing street MRT and cooling loads
(2026) Building and Environment, 297, art. no. 114479, Cited 0 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.buildenv.2026.114479
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105035388547?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: Departamento de Construcciones Arquitectónicas 1, Instituto Universitario de Arquitectura y Ciencias de la Construcción, Universidad de Sevilla, C/Reina Mercedes 2, Sevilla, 41012, Spain; Institute of Architecture and Technology, Royal Danish Academy Royal Danish Academy, Philip de Langes Allé 10, Copenhagen, 1435, Denmark; Department of Engineering and Architecture, Università di Parma, Parma, 43125, Italy; Norman Foster Institute, Norman Foster Foundation, Calle de Monte Esquinza 22, Madrid, 28010, Spain; Scuola di Architettura e Design "E. Vittoria", Università di Camerino, Ascoli Piceno, Italy
ABSTRACT: Both horizontal and vertical surface characteristics shape urban microclimates in hot‑summer cities. Although recent attention has turned to three‑dimensional (3D) façade geometries, their simultaneous quantitative impact on both building cooling energy and pedestrian thermal comfort has not been established. We simulate five façade morphologies, flat, pixelated, wedge, carved, and wavy, within a controlled Mediterranean canyon (H/W = 1.0, Nicosia, Cyprus) and compare summer heat conditions. A Honeybee–EnergyPlus–Radiance workflow, bench‑checked under laboratory conditions (MBE + 0.06 °C; CV(RMSE) 3 %), quantifies peak wall surface temperature, pavement conditions, and pedestrian‑level mean radiant temperature (MRT), Universal Thermal Climate Index (UTCI) hours and buildings’ annual cooling energy. 3D façades reduced peak wall temperature by up to 8.5 °C and annual cooling energy by 9–16 % under the current climate, with energy benefits narrowing to 6–9 % under RCP 8.5. Orientation is critical: in north–south canyons, 3D forms lowered peak pedestrian MRT by 4.7 °C and reduced UTCI “Very Strong” exposure by 9 percentage points. To contextualise the magnitude of these geometry driven effects, the discussion reports indicative ranges from established façade scale passive strategies. Compared with material‑based interventions, 3D forms slightly outperformed green façades (cooling efficiency up to 16 % vs 7–15 %) and matched retroreflective coatings for pedestrian comfort (MRT relief of 3–5 °C). These findings provide an evidence‑based foundation for the orientation‑specific deployment of 3D geometry as a passive, climate‑responsive envelope strategy. © 2026

High-performance envelopes in the Mediterranean: Energy and comfort effects of BIPVs, shading devices, and green walls
(2026) Energy and Buildings, 358, art. no. 117222, Cited 0 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.enbuild.2026.117222
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105031888860?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: İzmir Institute of Technology, Department of Architecture, Gülbahçe Campus, Urla, İzmir, 35430, Turkey
ABSTRACT: This study addresses the challenge of improving building envelope performance in Mediterranean climates, where dynamic seasonal changes require innovative solutions. The review examines the potential of high-performance building envelope applications, including building-integrated photovoltaics (BIPVs), shading devices, and vertical green walls, to enhance energy efficiency, comfort, and minimize environmental impact. While previous studies have predominantly focused on individual systems, this review considers a holistic approach by examining both separate and combined applications. The findings indicate that BIPV systems significantly enhance energy production, while shading devices mitigate overheating and enhance thermal and visual comfort. Additionally, vertical green walls enhance building performance by providing insulation and contributing to environmental benefits. The integration of these systems demonstrates synergistic potential to achieve multiple performance objectives, including energy savings, enhanced indoor comfort, and reduced emissions. However, the review identifies critical gaps in the literature, such as a lack of integration of holistic systems, limited consideration of future climate scenarios, and underutilization of optimization algorithms and machine learning in high-performance building envelope studies. Future research should lead to the development of adaptive design strategies, modular façade systems, holistic use of various applications, and incorporation of future climate predictions to ensure long-term sustainability. This study highlights the importance of developing integrated, high-performance building envelopes that adapt to changing environmental and climatic conditions. In conclusion, this review offers a comprehensive framework for advancing sustainable building practices in Mediterranean climates, providing valuable insights for architects and engineers seeking to create energy-efficient and resilient built environments. © 2026 Elsevier B.V.

Yao Q., Zhao K., Zhou Y., Ge J.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Yao, Qintong (60034637900); Zhao, Kang (56482651600); Zhou, Yanan (60034773700); Ge, Jian (48861340600)

Thermal performance and climate applicability analysis of multiple building envelopes
(2026) Applied Thermal Engineering, 283, art. no. 128924, Cited 2 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.applthermaleng.2025.128924
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105021081103?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: Department of Architecture, Zhejiang University, Hangzhou, 310058, China; Center for Balance Architecture, Architectural Design & Research Institute of Zhejiang University, Hangzhou, 310058, China; International Research Center for Green Building and Low-Carbon City, Zhejiang University, International Campus, Haining, 314400, China
ABSTRACT: The building envelope has a significant effect on its overall energy consumption. Integrating advanced techniques, such as phase change material (PCM), dynamic insulation materials and systems (DIMS), and pipe-embedded technology, into envelope design can significantly reduce heating and cooling loads. However, the energy-saving performance of these technologies varies considerably across different climatic conditions. In this study, five wall types incorporating various combinations of PCM, DIMS, and embedded pipes were modeled in COMSOL to evaluate their thermal performance and climate adaptability across different regions. In Hangzhou, results showed that during peak summer, PCM alone had a limited effect. In contrast, dynamic insulation achieved much higher savings, and the combined system reduced cooling load by up to 84%, showing the most significant potential. Further analysis revealed that different wall types exhibit varying sensitivities to climatic conditions, with day temperature having the most critical impact, explaining over 69 % of the cooling load variance in non-pipe-embedded walls. Regression analysis quantified the sensitivity of each wall type to key climatic parameters, and clustering analysis identified the most applicable wall type for each climate zone. Across 30 cities, cooling load reductions varied greatly, ranging from below 5 % to nearly 100 %, across different climatic groups, highlighting the strong climate dependence of wall applicability. The study quantitatively links climatic parameters with wall performance, providing a rational basis for climate-adaptive envelope design. © 2025 Elsevier Ltd.

Osman O., Yehya A., Maalouf E.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Osman, O. (59485944200); Yehya, A. (56638364600); Maalouf, E. (57194180469)
59485944200; 56638364600; 57194180469
Controlled ventilated phase change material façades for climate-responsive buildings
(2025) Journal of Energy Storage, 138, art. no. 118654, Cited 1 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.est.2025.118654
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105017673516?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: Department of Mechanical Engineering, Maroun Semaan Faculty of Engineering and Architecture, American University of Beirut, Lebanon; Department of Civil and Environmental Engineering, Maroun Semaan Faculty of Engineering and Architecture, American University of Beirut, Beirut, Lebanon; Baha and Walid Bassatne Department of Chemical Engineering and Advanced Energy, Maroun Semaan Faculty of Engineering and Architecture, American University of Beirut, Lebanon
ABSTRACT: Enhancing the thermal performance of phase change materials (PCM) has become an essential strategy for improving building envelope efficiency; however, effectively managing the charging and discharging cycles of PCM remains critical to maximizing their benefits. This study explores a promising novel solution, which is the use of controlled ventilation within PCM-enhanced wall assemblies (VPCM). Results demonstrated that the VPCM system consistently maintained inner surface temperatures between 23 and 24 °C, significantly outperforming conventional wall designs, which fluctuated from 23.5 to 26.5 °C. Cooling efficiency was notably improved, with gains of up to 75 % compared to traditional PCM-based walls. A detailed parametric analysis further highlighted key factors such as inlet air temperature, airflow velocity, pipe dimension, PCM melting temperature, PCM thickness, and PCM positioning that strongly influence overall system performance. Specifically, reducing inlet air temperature from 24 °C to 18 °C increased cooling efficiency from approximately 50 % to 77 %, and optimal pipe sizing boosted total efficiency from 18 % to 63 %. Alternative wall configurations, including dual inlet-outlet setups and pre-insulation placement, further increased total efficiency to 84 %, though these enhancements were accompanied by a cooling efficiency decline from 76 % to 63 %. Overall, the results underscore the substantial benefits of actively ventilated PCM systems, emphasizing the critical role of controlled ventilation in optimizing thermal comfort and energy performance within building envelopes. This work provides a scalable pathway toward climate-responsive and low-energy building envelopes, offering practical design strategies that can be integrated into sustainable construction and retrofit practices. © 2025 Elsevier Ltd

AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Sun, Yu (60613491100); Yang, Feining (59727797100); Xiao, Yuling (58039326900); Wang, Dong (59937899300); Zhou, Huangping (59937540500); Zhang, Lili (55264878900)
60613491100; 59727797100; 58039326900; 59937899300; 59937540500; 55264878900
Comprehensive evaluation of building energy load, power generation, and shading effects of vertical single-axis tracking PV louver systems
(2026) Applied Thermal Engineering, 299, art. no. 131205, Cited 0 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.applthermaleng.2026.131205
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105037762368?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: China MCC5 Group Corp., Ltd., Chengdu, 611830, China; College of Architectural and Urban-Rural Planning, Sichuan Agricultural University, Sichuan, Dujiangyan, 611830, China
ABSTRACT: Vertical Building-Integrated Photovoltaics (BIPV) offer a promising pathway for decarbonizing high-rise buildings, yet optimizing the trade-off between power generation and thermal load management remains challenging in Hot Summer and Cold Winter (HSCW) climates. This study proposes a vertical single-axis tracking PV louver system and establishes a hybrid simulation framework that couples Radiance for optical-electrical modeling with a high-fidelity XGBoost surrogate thermal model to predict annual building performance efficiently. Taking a representative office in Chengdu as a case study, the research systematically evaluates the energy impacts of array density, static tilt angles, and dynamic tracking strategies. Results demonstrate that maximizing louver density yields optimal net energy performance despite increased mutual shading. Compared to the optimal fixed-angle configuration (EUI = 56.1 kWh/m2), introducing solar tracking significantly reduces energy consumption. The Rule-Based Dynamic System achieved the theoretical minimum EUI of 44.1 kWh/m2, representing a 59.4% reduction relative to the baseline. The results show that, while solar tracking maximizes power generation by aligning panels perpendicular to the low-angle winter sun, it simultaneously acts as a shading barrier that obstructs beneficial passive heat gain, exacerbating heating loads. However, optimization analysis reveals that the Rule-Based Dynamic System defaults to the solar-tracking mode for the vast majority of the year, indicating that the value of electricity generation outweighs passive heat gain during most operational hours. Consequently, the performance advantage of the complex Dynamic System over the continuous Pure Solar-Tracking System (EUI = 44.3 kWh/m2) is marginal (0.2 kWh/m2). Considering the diminishing returns of acquiring passive heat versus high-grade electricity in cooling-dominated regions, the Pure Solar-Tracking System is recommended as the most robust and pragmatically optimal solution for vertical facades. © 2026 Elsevier Ltd.

AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Qi, Zongxin (59178005400); Li, Jiaxin (57868240900); Yu, Menghui (59384767200); Ma, Qingsong (57193546485); Bart, Dewancker (14029753600); Gao, Weijun (55505206200)
59178005400; 57868240900; 59384767200; 57193546485; 14029753600; 55505206200
Segmented dynamic photovoltaic shading façade based on kiri-origami: Multi-objective decision-making and interpretable machine learning analysis
(2026) Building and Environment, 296, art. no. 114519, Cited 0 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.buildenv.2026.114519
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105033708421?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: College of Architecture and Urban Planning, Qingdao University of Technology, Qingdao, 266033, China; Department of Architecture, The University of Kitakyushu, Kitakyushu, 808-0135, Japan
ABSTRACT: With rising demands for building energy conservation and indoor quality, photovoltaic shading facades (PVSFs) attract attention for radiation control and power generation. However, current designs struggle with synergistic optimization, photovoltaic (PV) self-shading, and high actuation energy. Existing analytical frameworks are constrained by neglecting view quality (VQ), decision redundancy, and insufficient mechanism analysis. Therefore, inspired by kiri-origami, this study proposes a novel segmented dynamic PVSF that utilizes single-sided stretching to achieve partitioned collaborative optimization (upper light guiding, lower PV shading) while mitigating PV self-shading. Concurrently, a framework integrating interpretable machine learning (IML) and criteria importance through intercriteria correlation (CRITIC) analyzes PVSF improvements in VQ, energy consumption, and daylighting. Results indicate the static PVSF selected via multi-objective decision making (MODM) maintains low PV self-shading (5.7%) and limited view obstruction, increasing useful daylight illuminance (UDI100–3000) by 7.4% and reducing net energy use intensity (net EUI) by 19.9% versus baseline. Furthermore, dynamic seasonal strategies (larger summer/smaller winter stretching angles) further optimize UDI100–3000, net EUI, VQ, and PV self-shading by 1.2%, 4.9%, 4.4%, and 7%, respectively. Subsequent climate adaptability analysis indicates that the system maintains low PV self-shading and optimizes daylighting and energy consumption in different climates, with dynamic strategies further enhancing performance. Finally, sensitivity analysis reveals that across climate zones (2A-6A), shading density gradually emerges as the dominant factor for energy efficiency, while component distribution replaces it as the primary factor for daylighting. Thus, this system offers a viable pathway for building-integrated photovoltaics (BIPV), while the framework enhances decision-making accuracy. © 2026 Elsevier Ltd

AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Du, Xuwei (59708610700); Yao, Peng (57218530355); Qu, Shuoshuo (57207275580); Wang, Qilin (57199479088); Zhou, Peifa (57218139117); Liu, Xiang (57223598117); Wang, Tingting (60125317500); Sun, Yang (59707439800); Chu, Dongkai (56643982900); Huang, Chuanzhen (8259805100)

Real-time monitoring of grinding wheel using a dynamic envelope model
(2026) Precision Engineering, 100, pp. 39 - 51, Cited 0 times.
DOI: 10.1016/j.precisioneng.2026.02.017
https://www.scopus.com/pages/publications/105031629892?origin=resultslist

AFFILIATIONS: Center for Advanced Jet Engineering Technologies (CaJET), School of Mechanical Engineering, Shandong University, Shandong, Jinan, 250061, China; Key Laboratory of High Efficiency and Clean Mechanical Manufacture, Shandong University, Ministry of Education, Shandong, Jinan, 250061, China; Yanshan University, Hebei, Qinhuangdao, 066000, China
ABSTRACT: In the grinding process, measuring the profile of the grinding wheel is important for evaluating the accuracy of machining the workpiece, monitoring the wear of the grinding wheel and compensating for machining errors. Vision-based monitoring methods are more accurate and intuitive than traditional methods. Therefore, this paper proposes an automatic grinding wheel profile monitoring method based on the dynamic wheel envelope. A sub-pixel edge detection method based on omnidirectional gradient is proposed, which expands the gradient direction of the image to any direction by constructing a variable image window. The high-precision localization of sub-pixel edges is achieved by normalizing the window, bringing the edge detection accuracy to the sub-pixel level. The idea of dimensionality reduction and parameter space decomposition is introduced into the Hough circle transformation to obtain outer circle pixels for coarse edge localization, and then the Tukey weight function is used to iteratively fit the circle to locate the precise edge of the dynamic grinding wheel envelope. A dynamic real-time detection algorithm for grinding wheel wear was implemented through the design of an omnidirectional gradient sub-pixel edge detection algorithm and Turkey's improved Hough transform fitting algorithm. The proposed wheel profile monitoring method was validated on a high-precision motion platform. As the rotational speed increased, the radius deviation of the wheel was reduced from ±0.56 μm to ±0.24 μm, and the maximum radius deviation was reduced from less than 0.13% to less than 0.03% of the nominal radius. The graphite plate replica method was also used to verify the accuracy of the measurements, and the results demonstrated the feasibility and accuracy of the method for the rapid, non-contact measurement and monitoring of grinding wheel profiles. © 2026 Elsevier Inc.

Zhang H., Wang Z., Ye B., Zhou Y., Liu X., Zhang Y., Shen P.
AUTHOR FULL NAMES: Zhang, Hao (60630692300); Wang, Zheng (58718910600); Ye, Bicui (56242640000); Zhou, Yuekuan (57191995034); Liu, Xue (57211075387); Zhang, Yi (60630692400); Shen, Pengyuan (54400115300)
